# PHASE 2 — PER-FORMULA LEDGER

**Status** FIRST PASS. **8.7 % of the required determinations are made; 91.3 % are UNVERIFIED.**
Nothing in this file marks a Phase-2 requirement COMPLETE.

P02-107 requires a verdict per formula and P02-108-111 fix the vocabulary to
**PASS / FAIL / WARNING / UNVERIFIED**. P02-112 forbids *"probably correct"*. This ledger
therefore assigns a verdict ONLY where evidence already held determines one, and UNVERIFIED
with a stated reason everywhere else. **UNVERIFIED here means not yet examined, not benign.**

**Scope** the **1,288 retained engineering relations** (register class C1) x the **20 questions**
of P02-087-106 = **25,760 determinations** required.

**Tooling** `scratchpad/reg_parse.mjs` + `build_p2_ledger.mjs` + `emit_ledger.mjs`. Neither runs
the engine nor inspects any formula's numerical output, so no part of this is self-certifying.
The register is parsed by taking the columns before `equation as coded` from the left and those
after it from the right, because equations legitimately contain `||` and a naive split is corrupt
(N-21). The parse is validated against the controlled vocabularies of the trailing columns:
`confidence` resolves to exactly 3 values, `impl` to 1, `unitVerif` to 5, and the `unitVerif`
split reproduces the register's independently recorded 165/198/135/6/784 exactly.

## 2. What the twelve answered questions rest on

- **Q3 units (P02-089)** — PASS where the register resolved the units and dimensional
  propagation found them consistent (DECLARED or DERIVED). **FAIL on the 6 rows carrying a
  recorded dimensional CONFLICT.** UNVERIFIED for AMBIGUOUS and UNRESOLVED, which is the
  **28.2 % units-resolution cap**; its only lever is source-side naming, a production change
  that is not authorised (N-18).
- **Q9 duplication (P02-095)** — fully determined, the only question that is. Decided by exact
  equation-shape match inside the retained set. Note this asks only about duplication WITHIN
  the retained relations; the register's 422 C7 sites are duplicate implementations that were
  excluded from C1 by the classification rule, and are therefore not re-counted here.
- **Q10 coefficient sourcing (P02-096)** — PASS on DIRECTLY STATED and MEASURED FROM SOURCE.
  **WARNING on the 25 rows declared an ENGINEERING ASSUMPTION**: declared is not sourced.
  UNVERIFIED on the **944 rows (73.3 %) whose source is UNVERIFIED** — this figure was carried
  forward unverified in N-21 and is **now re-derived and confirmed** at 944/1288.
- **Q20 zero/negative/NaN (P02-106)** — **ASYMMETRIC BY DESIGN, and it yields no PASS.** The
  absence of any guard near a site is evidence the case is unhandled, so it is recorded as a
  **WARNING (253 relations)**. The presence of a guard does NOT show that it covers this
  relation, so it yields UNVERIFIED rather than PASS. A mechanical scan cannot earn a PASS here.
  **Three of the 253 are hand-verified rather than scanned** (`gearlayout.js` l.173, 176, 181):
  the zero-denominator fallback there does catch 0, but NaN is falsy, so a NaN *numerator* passes
  straight through to the output. Mutation returns NaN, and an undefined nose-wheel count returns
  a plausible-looking 1,137.2 kg against an 81.6 kg baseline. A guard that is present and does not
  cover the case is the WARNING condition, not the UNVERIFIED one. Latent, not live:
  `landinggear.js` always supplies the wheel counts.

- **Q1, Q4, Q5 and Q11 — hand-audited, not scanned, and only where the evidence is in hand.** The
  first determinations on these four questions come from the `src/engine/gearlayout.js` audit:
  **19 relations, 48 determinations**, each traceable to a stated basis in its row's notes and to a
  probe kept in the session scratchpad. Nothing here is mechanical, and nothing outside that one
  file is claimed. **Q10 was deliberately not touched**: the gate binds it to the register's
  `source` column with no escape clause, the register records those rows UNVERIFIED, and editing
  the register so that it agrees with a conclusion drawn from it is the F-42 error.
  - **Q1 mathematical expression (P02-087)** — **19 PASS**, earned three ways: exhaustive
    enumeration over a closed domain (the tyre table, 11 of 11 designations parse to the correct
    section width), algebra (the moment balance `x_main = (x_cg - f x_nose) / (1 - f)` inverted
    correctly), and an exact identity (the mass partition reproduces `landingGear().mass` to
    1.9e-16 on all six configurations).
  - **Q4 sign convention (P02-090)** — **17 PASS**: x measured aft from the nose and z positive up,
    with `x_nose < x_cg < x_main` and static clearance > 0 on all six configurations. The two
    tyre-width relations are **left UNVERIFIED on purpose** — a section width is a positive
    magnitude carrying no sign convention, and a vacuous PASS is still an unearned determination.
  - **Q5 reference conditions (P02-091)** — **1 WARNING**. The vertical criterion requires static
    clearance to cover the fitted strut stroke, but `landinggear.js`'s energy balance treats tyre
    deflection as a **separate co-absorber**, so at full compression the aircraft descends by the
    stroke *plus* the tyre deflection. WARNING and not FAIL, because that term is never the binding
    one: the strut-length term binds on all six configurations with 0.18-0.23 m of margin, and no
    configuration strikes the ground. **An incomplete criterion is not a failure.**
  - **Q11 hidden double-counting (P02-097)** — **8 PASS**, on the mass relations only, where the
    partition identity is the evidence. The geometric relations are left UNVERIFIED because the
    question does not bite on them and nothing measured here would settle it.

## 3. The two register columns that already state Phase 2 is undone

Independent of anything measured here, the register itself records for **all 1,288** relations:

- `validation` = **"NOT VALIDATED — Phase 2"**
- `indep verif` = **"NO — no independent calculation"**

So Phase 2 has no independently recomputed relation anywhere in the engine. That is consistent
with 0 of 112 requirements COMPLETE and with the no-self-certification rule: an independent
calculation is exactly what has not been done.

| FORMULA-STRUCT-089 | `src/classes/aircraft/fuselage-shear-flow.js:97` | P | P | U | P | P | U | W | U | P | P | U | U | U | U | U | U | P | U | P | W | 9: equation shape is unique in the retained set; 10: source DIRECTLY STATED; 1: q = (S_y / pi R) cos(theta) re-derived independently from axial equilibrium rather than read off the module - I = int y^2 t ds = pi R^3 t, dq/dtheta = -(S_y/I) t R^2 sin(theta) integrates to (S_y/pi R) cos(theta), and the closing constant vanishes by symmetry about the load axis. Matches line 97 exactly. The resultant int q dy = S_y and int q dx = 0 were confirmed analytically and numerically; 2: shearN is F1's transverse shear S_y at the station and radiusM is F2's equivalent-area circle radius, both held by identity - the gate compares them to F1 and F2 directly. theta's datum is declared at line 95 as the starboard horizontal and the implementation is consistent with it; 4: verified by direction rather than by inspection - for S_y > 0 the flow is positive at theta = 0, and a negative shear reverses it and returns Fy = -S_y exactly. The resultant matches the applied shear in magnitude AND direction at every boom count tested, 3 to 65; 5: the reference datum for theta is declared at line 95 as the starboard horizontal and the implementation is consistent with it - the flow is largest at theta = 0 on the neutral axis and zero at +/- pi/2 on the load axis. Note that the research note FUSELAGE-STRUCTURE.md section 2.1 states the same falsifiable check as (S_y/pi R) sin(theta): that is the identical function measured from the vertical, and the note's own build record states the cos form the module implements, so the datum is documented rather than in conflict; 7: WARNING - two validity conditions, one met and one not. Thin wall is comfortably met, re-measured this block at R/t_skin 1206.9 transport, 886.0 bizjet, 1401.4 turboprop. The CIRCULAR section is not: the ring rests on (A.13), which TASOPT states with the explicit proviso '(if w_db = 0, dR_fuse = 0)', and this tool's section is an ellipse - re-measured independently at H/W 1.0560 transport, 1.0457 bizjet, 1.1007 turboprop, i.e. 4.6 to 10.1 per cent out of round - for which an equivalent-area circle is substituted. The same departure F2 carries, and the module declares it rather than hiding it, but a declared departure from a stated validity condition is not the condition being met; 17: not circular - F4 is a terminal consumer. It reads F1 and F2 and is itself imported only by tabs.jsx:709 for display; it sizes nothing, returns no mass, energy or geometry, and feeds nothing back into the loop that produced its inputs; 19: exactly homogeneous, so scaling cannot degrade it - measured q_max/S_y constant to 13 significant figures over six decades of S_y, q_max x R constant under R x 10, and q_max invariant under t x 100 because the t in the numerator cancels the t in I. No subtraction of near-equal terms anywhere in this relation; 20: WARNING - hand-verified, not scanned. The module carries no finiteness, zero or negative guard anywhere between lines 96 and 152 - the only numeric clamp is max(3, floor(nBooms)) on the count, and the ?? fallbacks at lines 157-166 are chain-level refusals naming an upstream module, not input guards. thinRingShearFlow returns Infinity at radiusM 0, and NaN at radiusM 0 with shearN 0 |
| FORMULA-STRUCT-090 | `src/classes/aircraft/fuselage-shear-flow.js:107` | P | P | U | P | U | U | W | U | P | U | P | U | U | U | U | U | P | U | P | W | 9: equation shape is unique in the retained set; 1: B = 2 pi R t_shell / n. The identity it exists to satisfy, sum B y_r^2 = pi R^3 t_shell, was proved analytically for EVERY n >= 3 - sum sin^2(theta_r) = n/2 because sum cos(2 pi (2r+1)/n) vanishes for n >= 3 - and confirmed numerically at the clamp floor n = 3 as well as 4 and 5, rel 2.5e-16. The module claims n >= 3 and the harness tests only n >= 4; 2: B is a boom AREA in m^2, R the ring radius, t_shell the (A.7) bending-effective thickness. The t_shell binding is the correct one here because the booms carry the axial path, which is the path (A.7) credits stringers to; 4: positive for positive R and t_shell, as a boom area must be. A negative radius propagates a negative area rather than being rejected, which is recorded under question 20; 7: WARNING - two validity conditions, one met and one not. Thin wall is comfortably met, re-measured this block at R/t_skin 1206.9 transport, 886.0 bizjet, 1401.4 turboprop. The CIRCULAR section is not: the ring rests on (A.13), which TASOPT states with the explicit proviso '(if w_db = 0, dR_fuse = 0)', and this tool's section is an ellipse - re-measured independently at H/W 1.0560 transport, 1.0457 bizjet, 1.1007 turboprop, i.e. 4.6 to 10.1 per cent out of round - for which an equivalent-area circle is substituted. The same departure F2 carries, and the module declares it rather than hiding it, but a declared departure from a stated validity condition is not the condition being met; 11: the axial and shear paths are disjoint by construction and no material is credited to both - t_shell carries the booms and the bending inertia, t_skin alone carries the shear stress, exactly as (A.7) licenses. Confirmed behaviourally as well as structurally: crediting stringers at 0.35 moves the boom areas and leaves the peak flow and the peak stress unchanged to rel < 1e-9; 17: not circular - F4 is a terminal consumer. It reads F1 and F2 and is itself imported only by tabs.jsx:709 for display; it sizes nothing, returns no mass, energy or geometry, and feeds nothing back into the loop that produced its inputs; 19: exactly homogeneous, so scaling cannot degrade it - measured q_max/S_y constant to 13 significant figures over six decades of S_y, q_max x R constant under R x 10, and q_max invariant under t x 100 because the t in the numerator cancels the t in I. No subtraction of near-equal terms anywhere in this relation; 20: WARNING - hand-verified, not scanned. The module carries no finiteness, zero or negative guard anywhere between lines 96 and 152 - the only numeric clamp is max(3, floor(nBooms)) on the count, and the ?? fallbacks at lines 157-166 are chain-level refusals naming an upstream module, not input guards. t_shell 0 makes B 0, hence the boom inertia 0, and every flow downstream NaN |

Verified against the primary: Goodyear 2018 radial table row 461B-3946-TL prints 20.40 and 4.65 under Grown Max beside 19.80 and 4.10 under the shoulder Dia Max and Width Max, and tyres.js carries odMaxIn 20.4 and widthMaxIn 4.65 - the GROWN columns. H = (D_O - D)/2 recomputed longhand, rel 0.0e+0; 2: D is the specified rim diameter, W_G and D_G the table's grown section width and outside diameter, H the section height. The binding is the substantive claim here and it is verified row by row against the book, not assumed - see question 11; 4: every returned dimension is positive on the valid domain, and the smallest D_O - D margin across the 294-row database is 6.70 in. A row inverting that order is accepted rather than refused, recorded under question 20; 5: the reference condition is WHICH dimensions the table prints - grown or ungrown - and it is verified rather than assumed. The Goodyear radial table prints Grown Max outside diameter and section width (20.40 and 4.65 for 461B-3946-TL) alongside the ungrown shoulder columns (19.80 and 4.10), and the generated database carries the grown pair. So the module's reading, that growth must not be applied a second time, is correct against the primary; 11: the live double-counting risk in this module, and it is resolved with evidence. Applying the bias growth (32)-(33) to a radial whose table values are already grown would count the growth twice. Measured on the 27 radial rows, a second growth would add 0.198 to 0.402 in of bay half-height, 1.95 to 3.03 per cent of D_G. The module declines it, and the primary supports declining it: the table's Grown Max columns are what the database carries. The bias path applies G_W and G_H exactly once each, and clearances reads W_G from the envelope rather than regrowing it, so the two cannot drift apart; 17: not circular - G7 is terminal. stowage is written into gear.js's return at l.166 and read by no sizing path; the only other mentions of it in the tree are comments. The tyre it rests on is selected from the load, which descends from MTOW, so the dependency runs one way and nothing stowage computes re-enters the loop that produced its inputs. It returns no mass and no energy; 19: homogeneous of degree 1 in length, so a consistent rescale cannot degrade it - verified by rescaling the whole tyre row by 0.0254 and recovering D_G to rel 0.0e+0. AR and the growth factors are dimensionless; 20: WARNING - hand-verified, not scanned. The guard at l.64 requires D, W and D_O finite and positive but never requires D_O > D, so an inverted row is accepted: forcing odMaxIn 5 against rimDiaIn 12 returns resolved true with D_G 5 and H -3.5, and on the bias path H = -1 with D_G 2.909, neither refused. See F-56 |

| FORMULA-STRUCT-173 | `src/engine/gear/stowage.js:90` | P | P | U | P | W | U | W | U | P | U | U | U | U | U | U | U | P | U | W | W | 9: equation shape is unique in the retained set; 1: (39) the radial-only envelope C_R = 0.029 (D_G - D)^0.5 (W_G - A)^0.5 (SPEED/D_G)^0.5 + 0.15, Goodyear 2018 p. 22. Recomputed longhand on all 27 radial rows at their rated speeds, rel 0.0e+0; R_x, W_x and S_x from (41) likewise rel 0.0e+0; 2: A is the rim width BETWEEN FLANGES, which is the book's own column of that name (3.50 in for 461B-3946-TL) and is carried as rimWidthIn; it is not the section width and not the rim diameter. SPEED is in mph and D_G in inches; 4: C_R is a positive outward clearance; the additive 0.15 keeps it positive even as the root term vanishes; 5: WARNING - the T&RA rule states SPEED as the aircraft's ground speed. The module defaults instead to the selected tyre's RATED speed (l.122) and records which was used in speed.basis, and the caveat says a real bay is checked at the aircraft's maximum ground speed. The substitution is CONSERVATIVE, the rated speed being the largest the tyre may be run to, and it is declared rather than hidden - but a declared substitution for a stated reference condition is not that condition being met. Measured: the database's rated speeds run 104 to 262 mph; 7: WARNING - the source prints no validity range for the speed term and the module enforces none. The bias exponent 3.348 makes C_R strongly superlinear: the 2.61 (V/100)^3.348 term is 2.61 at 100 mph, 39.42 at 225 and 59.94 at 255, and a caller-supplied speed is unbounded, so 400 mph would silently return 3.2763 in of radial clearance on a 10 in grown width. The database's own rated speeds span 104 to 262 mph, so nothing that ships leaves the fitted region, but nothing stops a caller from doing so; 17: not circular - G7 is terminal. stowage is written into gear.js's return at l.166 and read by no sizing path; the only other mentions of it in the tree are comments. The tyre it rests on is selected from the load, which descends from MTOW, so the dependency runs one way and nothing stowage computes re-enters the loop that produced its inputs. It returns no mass and no energy; 19: WARNING - this is a DIMENSIONAL empirical fit, not a homogeneous relation. The additive constants 0.4, 0.23, 0.15 and the 0.10 floor are all in inches and SPEED is in mph, so the relation is scale-bound by construction and a caller working in metres is silently wrong rather than refused. Measured: rescaling the row and the rim width by 0.0254 gives R_x 0.5885 against the correct 7.8205 in, a ratio of 13.289 where a homogeneous relation would give 39.37. The speedMph and rimWidthIn parameters name their units, but the envelope argument's D, D_G and W_G and the returned C_R, C_W, R_x, W_x and S_x carry no unit in their names - which is the same source-side naming gap N-18 records, and is why the register leaves these rows AMBIGUOUS or UNRESOLVED; 20: WARNING - hand-verified, and the guarding is ASYMMETRIC. Line 90 takes two square roots; sqrt(W_G - A) is guarded at l.87 and sqrt(D_G - D) is not. Forcing a radial to odMaxIn 5 against rimDiaIn 12 gives D_G - D = -7 and returns C_R = NaN and R_x = NaN, which propagate unannounced into bayHalfHeightIn and crossSectionM2. Latent: 0 of 294 database rows invert that order. See F-56 |
